{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XKw8GQXKhgEKu1v45ySCEJ","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# MedicalMine Inc. — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-15\n     4→Developer website: https://www.charmhealth.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→MedicalMine Inc. is a privately held health IT company founded in 2007 by Dr. Pramila Srinivasan (PhD, Purdue), based in Pleasanton, California. The company operates under the brand name **CharmHealth** and builds a cloud-based, all-in-one healthcare platform targeting ambulatory practices — particularly independent clinics, small-to-medium practices, and integrative/functional medicine providers. The company has approximately 23 employees (as of late 2023) and has not disclosed external funding rounds. At least 5,000 licensed physicians are active CharmHealth EHR users (based on a 2023 CharmHealthSquare community launch announcement referencing \"approximately 5,000 licensed physicians who are existing CharmHealth EHR users\"). The company has a medical advisory board of 13+ physicians across multiple specialties.\n     9→\n    10→CharmHealth has expanded internationally through a partnership with EMDI to distribute in the Gulf Cooperation Council (GCC) region, and in 2022 announced the CharmHealth + Bioverge Digital Transformation Fund, a venture fund focused on early-stage digital health companies. The product is marketed as \"founded and designed by doctors\" and has been recognized as \"Best Free EMR of 2024\" by Software Advice. It is hosted on Zoho's cloud infrastructure.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: CharmHealth EHR\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 9741\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→CharmHealth EHR is a cloud-based, ONC-certified electronic health record system that is part of a broader integrated suite including practice management, revenue cycle management, patient engagement portal, telehealth, secure messaging (CharmConnect), and an app marketplace (CharmHealthHub). The certified module (CharmHealth EHR v1.2, certified 2018-11-15) appears to be the clinical EHR component, but the broader product under which it operates encompasses billing, scheduling, patient portal, telehealth, and more. The CHPL certification spans a broad set of criteria: clinical data capabilities (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); patient portal (e)(1), (e)(3); public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(2); FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(10); and direct messaging (h)(1). The SED intended user description is \"Outpatient Clinic.\"\n    19→\n    20→Integrated third-party components listed on the mandatory disclosures page: AccessGUDID (drug/device identification), Surescripts eRx (electronic prescribing), IronBridge PubHub (data management), and DataMotion Direct (secure messaging).\n    21→\n    22→### Users & Market\n    23→\n    24→CharmHealth targets independent and small-to-medium ambulatory practices. It has particular traction with **integrative medicine** and **functional medicine** providers — this is a distinguishing niche. The vendor lists supported specialties including family practice, urgent care, internal medicine, pediatrics, psychiatry, integrative medicine, gynecology, dental, bariatrics, cardiology, dermatology, emergency medicine, gastroenterology, and others.\n    25→\n    26→A case study on the CharmHealth website describes Capital Integrative Health, an integrative/primary care practice with 10 practitioners that grew from 0 to 930 patients within 7 months of implementing CharmHealth. Users in reviews frequently mention using it for integrative and functional medicine practices.\n    27→\n    28→Reviews on Capterra, GetApp, and FindEMR characterize CharmHealth as cost-effective and well-suited for smaller practices. Users appreciate the customizable templates and affordable pricing. Common criticisms include occasional system slowness, limited billing functionality compared to larger EHRs, and inconsistent customer support responsiveness.\n    29→\n    30→The pricing model includes a free tier (50 encounters/month, 1 provider, 5 users), an encounter-based plan ($0.50/encounter, $25/month minimum), and a provider-based plan ($200/provider/month). Add-ons are priced separately for e-prescribing ($10/provider/month), telehealth ($20/provider/month), document scanning ($10/month), eCommerce (1% transaction fee), and AI Scribe ($125/provider/month or $2/encounter).\n    31→\n    32→### Modules & Functionality\n    33→\n    34→Based on vendor website, feature pages, pricing tiers, case studies, and third-party review sites, CharmHealth includes the following modules and capabilities:\n    35→\n    36→**Clinical Documentation (EHR)**\n    37→- SOAP note templates customizable by specialty\n    38→- Image annotation tools\n    39→- QuickText connections for additional laboratories\n    56→- Lab results flow into patient records and patient portal\n    57→\n    58→**Immunization Registry**\n    59→- Electronic transmission of vaccine data to state registries (certified for (f)(1))\n    60→\n    61→**Practice Management**\n    62→- Multi-facility appointment scheduling with resource management (rooms, IV chairs, etc.)\n    63→- - Query/path parameters for each operation\n * - Sample request/response examples\n * - OAuth scopes\n * - Bulk export and CCDA API details\n */\n\nimport { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { join } from \"path\";\n\nconst INPUT_FILE = join(import.meta.dir, \"..\", \"fhir-api-documentation.html\");\nconst OUTPUT_FILE = join(import.meta.dir, \"fhir-api-extracted.json\");\n\nconst html = readFileSync(INPUT_FILE, \"utf-8\");\n\n// Simple HTML text extractor\nfunction stripHtml(s: string): string {\n  return s.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, \" \").replace(/\\s+/g, \" \").trim();\n}\n\n// Extract text between two patterns\nfunction extractBetween(content: string, startPattern: RegExp, endPattern: RegExp): string {\n  const startMatch = content.match(startPattern);\n  if (!startMatch) return \"\";\n  const startIdx = startMatch.index! + startMatch[0].length;\n  const rest = content.slice(startIdx);\n  const endMatch = rest.match(endPattern);\n  if (!endMatch) return rest;\n  return rest.slice(0, endMatch.index);\n}\n\n// Parse table rows into array of objects\nfunction parseTables(section: string): Array<Record<string, string>> {\n  const rows: Array<Record<string, string>> = [];\n  const tableMatch = section.match(/<table>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/);\n  if (!tableMatch) return rows;\n\n  const tableHtml = tableMatch[1];\n\n  // Extract headers\n  const headerMatch = tableHtml.match(/<thead>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/thead>/);\n  if (!headerMatch) return rows;\n\n  const headers: string[] = [];\n  const thRegex = /<th[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/th>/g;\n  let thMatch;\n  while ((thMatch = thRegex.exec(headerMatch[1])) !== null) {\n    headers.push(stripHtml(thMatch[1]).toLowerCase());\n  }\n  // Also check for <td> in thead (some tables use td for headers)\n  if (headers.length === 0) {\n    const tdRegex = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/g;\n    let tdMatch;\n    while ((tdMatch = tdRegex.exec(headerMatch[1])) !== null) {\n      headers.push(stripHtml(tdMatch[1]).toLowerCase());\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract body rows\n  const bodyMatch = tableHtml.match(/<tbody>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tbody>/);\n  if (!bodyMatch) return rows;\n\n  const trRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/g;\n  let trMatch;\n  while ((trMatch = trRegex.exec(bodyMatch[1])) !== null) {\n    const cells: string[] = [];\n    const tdRegex2 = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/g;\n    let tdMatch2;\n    while ((tdMatch2 = tdRegex2.exec(trMatch[1])) !== null) {\n      cells.push(stripHtml(tdMatch2[1]));\n    }\n    if (cells.length > 0 && headers.length > 0) {\n      const row: Record<string, string> = {};\n      headers.forEach((h, i) => {\n        if (i < cells.length) row[h] = cells[i];\n      });\n      rows.push(row);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return rows;\n}\n\n// Extract code blocks\nfunction extractCodeBlocks(section: string): string[] {\n  const blocks: string[] = [];\n  const codeRegex = /<code[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/code>/g;\n  let match;\n  while ((match = codeRegex.exec(section)) !== null) {\n    const text = stripHtml(match[1]).trim();\n    if (text.length > 10) blocks.push(text);\n  }\n  return blocks;\n}\n\n// Known FHIR resources from the page\nconst RESOURCE_NAMES = [\n  \"AllergyIntolerance\", \"Appointment\", \"CarePlan\", \"CareTeam\", \"Condition\",\n  \"Device\", \"DiagnosticReport\", \"DocumentReference\", \"Encounter\",\n  \"FamilyMemberHistory\", \"Goal\", \"Immunization\", \"Location\",\n  \"MedicationAdministration\", \"MedicationRequest\", \"Medication\",\n  \"Observation\", \"Organization\", \"Patient\", \"Practitioner\", \"Procedure\",\n  \"Provenance\", \"QuestionnaireResponse\", \"RelatedPerson\"\n];\n\ninterface Operation {\n  name: string;\n  type: \"list\" | \"get\" | \"search\";\n  apiUrl: string | null;\n  parameters: Array<Record<string, string>>;\n  requestExample: string | null;\n  responseExample: string | null;\n}\n\ninterface Resource {\n  name: string;\n  usCorProfile: string | null;\n  description: string | null;\n  operations: Operation[];\n}\n\n// Parse resources\nconst resources: Resource[] = [];\n\nfor (const resourceName of RESOURCE_NAMES) {\n  const lowerName = resourceName.toLowerCase();\n\n  // Find the resource header section\n  const headerPattern = new RegExp(`<h1\\\\s+id=\"${lowerName}\"[^>]*>[\\\\s\\\\S]*?</h1>`);\n  const headerMatch = html.match(headerPattern);\n  if (!headerMatch) continue;\n\n  const headerIdx = headerMatch.index!;\n\n  // Find description and US Core profile link\n  const nextSection = html.slice(headerIdx, headerIdx + 2000);\n  const descMatch = nextSection.match(/<p>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/p>/);\n  const description = descMatch ? stripHtml(descMatch[1]) : null;\n\n  const profileMatch = nextSection.match(/href=\"(http[^\"]*us[/-]core[^\"]*StructureDefinition[^\"]*)\"/i);\n  const usCorProfile = profileMatch ? profileMatch[1] : null;\n\n  // Find operations for this resource\n  const operations: Operation[] = [];\n\n  for (const opType of [\"list\", \"get\", \"search\"] as const) {\n    const opId = `${opType}-${lowerName}`;\n    const opPattern = new RegExp(`<h2\\\\s+id=\"${opId}\"[^>]*>`);\n    const opMatch = html.match(opPattern);\n    if (!opMatch) continue;\n\n    const opIdx = opMatch.index!;\n    // Find the next h1 or h2 to delimit the section\n    const opRest = html.slice(opIdx + opMatch[0].length);\n    const nextHeadingMatch = opRest.match(/<h[12]\\s/);\n    const opSection = nextHeadingMatch\n      ? opRest.slice(0, nextHeadingMatch.index)\n      : opRest.slice(0, 5000);\n\n    // Extract API URL\n    const urlMatch = opSection.match(/<code>\\s*((?:GET|POST|DELETE|PUT)\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\n    const apiUrl = urlMatch ? stripHtml(urlMatch[1]) : null;\n\n    // Extract parameters table\n    const parameters = parseTables(opSection);\n\n    // Extract code examples\n    const codeBlocks = extractCodeBlocks(opSection);\n    const requestExample = codeBlocks.find(b => b.includes(\"curl\")) || null;\n    const responseExample = codeBlocks.find(b => b.includes(\"resourceType\") || b.includes(\"HTTP/1.1\")) || null;\n\n    operations.push({\n      name: `${opType} ${resourceName}`,\n      type: opType,\n      apiUrl,\n      parameters,\n      requestExample,\n      responseExample,\n    });\n  }\n\n  // Special case for Medication which only has \"get\"\n  if (resourceName === \"Medication\" && operations.length === 0) {\n    const opPattern = /id=\"get-medication\"/;\n    const opMatch = html.match(opPattern);\n    if (opMatch) {\n      const opIdx = opMatch.index!;\n      const opRest = html.slice(opIdx);\n      const nextH = opRest.match(/<h[12]\\s/);\n      const opSection = nextH ? opRest.slice(0, nextH.index) : opRest.slice(0, 3000);\n\n      const urlMatch = opSection.match(/<code>\\s*(GET\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\n      operations.push({\n        name: \"get Medication\",\n        type: \"get\",\n        apiUrl: urlMatch ? stripHtml(urlMatch[1]) : null,\n        parameters: parseTables(opSection),\n        requestExample: null,\n        responseExample: null,\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  resources.push({\n    name: resourceName,\n    usCorProfile,\n    description,\n    operations,\n  });\n}\n\n// Extract OAuth scopes\nconst scopeSections = html.match(/<h3[^>]*id=\"patient-scope\"[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/);\nconst patientScopes = scopeSections ? parseTables(scopeSections[0]) : [];\n\nconst userScopeSections = html.match(/<h3[^>]*id=\"user-scope\"[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/);\nconst userScopes = userScopeSections ? parseTables(userScopeSections[0]) : [];\n\nconst systemScopeSections = html.match(/<h3[^>]*id=\"system-scope\"[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/);\nconst systemScopes = systemScopeSections ? parseTables(systemScopeSections[0]) : [];\n\n// Extract Bulk Export info\nconst bulkExportSection = extractBetween(html, /<h1\\s+id=\"bulk-export\"/, /<\\/div>\\s*<div class=\"dark-box\">/);\nconst bulkExportOps: { name: string; apiUrl: string | null; parameters: Array<Record<string, string>>; description: string | null }[] = [];\n\nfor (const op of [\n  { id: \"start-export\", name: \"Start Bulk Export\" },\n  { id: \"get-status\", name: \"Get Bulk Export Status\" },\n  { id: \"delete-export\", name: \"Delete Bulk Export Request\" }\n]) {\n  const opPattern = new RegExp(`<h2\\\\s+id=\"${op.id}\"[^>]*>`);\n  const opMatch = html.match(opPattern);\n  if (!opMatch) continue;\n\n  const opIdx = opMatch.index!;\n  const opRest = html.slice(opIdx + opMatch[0].length);\n  const nextH = opRest.match(/<h[12]\\s/);\n  const opSection = nextH ? opRest.slice(0, nextH.index) : opRest.slice(0, 3000);\n\n  const urlMatch = opSection.match(/<code>\\s*((?:GET|POST|DELETE)\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\n  const descMatch = opSection.match(/<p>([^<]*(?:export|Fetches|Deletes)[^<]*)<\\/p>/i);\n\n  bulkExportOps.push({\n    name: op.name,\n    apiUrl: urlMatch ? stripHtml(urlMatch[1]) : null,\n    parameters: parseTables(opSection),\n    description: descMatch ? stripHtml(descMatch[1]) : null,\n  });\n}\n\n// Extract CCDA section\nconst ccdaSection = extractBetween(html, /<h1\\s+id=\"ccda\"/, /<h1\\s+id=\"bulk-export\"/);\nconst ccdaUrlMatch = ccdaSection.match(/<code>\\s*(GET\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\nconst ccdaParams = parseTables(ccdaSection);\n\n// Build output\nconst output = {\n  source: \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/fhir/index.html\",\n  extractionDate: new Date().toISOString().split(\"T\")[0],\n  fhirVersion: \"4.0.1\",\n  baseUrl: \"https://ehr2.charmtracker.com/api/ehr/v2/fhir\",\n  summary: {\n    totalResources: resources.length,\n    resourceNames: resources.map(r => r.name),\n    totalOperations: resources.reduce((sum, r) => sum + r.operations.length, 0),\n    resourcesWithUsCorProfile: resources.filter(r => r.usCorProfile).length,\n  },\n  resources,\n  oauthScopes: {\n    patient: patientScopes,\n    user: userScopes,\n    system: systemScopes,\n  },\n  bulkExport: {\n    description: \"Exports data of more than one resource of the group of patients in NDJSON format\",\n    operations: bulkExportOps,\n  },\n  ccda: {\n    description: \"Export medical records of a patient in HL7 CCDA format\",\n    apiUrl: ccdaUrlMatch ? stripHtml(ccdaUrlMatch[1]) : null,\n    parameters: ccdaParams,\n  },\n  parsingStats: {\n    inputFile: \"fhir-api-documentation.html\",\n    inputSizeBytes: html.length,\n    resourcesParsed: resources.length,\n    resourcesExpected: RESOURCE_NAMES.length,\n    parseFailures: RESOURCE_NAMES.filter(n => !resources.find(r => r.name === n)).map(n => ({\n      resourceName: n,\n      reason: \"Header not found in HTML\"\n    })),\n  }\n};\n\nwriteFileSync(OUTPUT_FILE, JSON.stringify(output, null, 2));\nconsole.log(`Extracted ${resources.length} resources with ${output.summary.totalOperations} headerPattern = new RegExp(`<h1\\\\s+id=\"${lowerName}\"[^>]*>[\\\\s\\\\S]*?</h1>`);\n  const headerMatch = html.match(headerPattern);\n  if (!headerMatch) continue;\n\n  const headerIdx = headerMatch.index!;\n\n  // Find description and US Core profile link\n  const nextSection = html.slice(headerIdx, headerIdx + 2000);\n  const descMatch = nextSection.match(/<p>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/p>/);\n  const description = descMatch ? stripHtml(descMatch[1]) : null;\n\n  const profileMatch = nextSection.match(/href=\"(http[^\"]*us[/-]core[^\"]*StructureDefinition[^\"]*)\"/i);\n  const usCorProfile = profileMatch ? profileMatch[1] : null;\n\n  // Find operations for this resource\n  const operations: Operation[] = [];\n\n  for (const opType of [\"list\", \"get\", \"search\"] as const) {\n    const opId = `${opType}-${lowerName}`;\n    const opPattern = new RegExp(`<h2\\\\s+id=\"${opId}\"[^>]*>`);\n    const opMatch = html.match(opPattern);\n    if (!opMatch) continue;\n\n    const opIdx = opMatch.index!;\n    // Find the next h1 or h2 to delimit the section\n    const opRest = html.slice(opIdx + opMatch[0].length);\n    const nextHeadingMatch = opRest.match(/<h[12]\\s/);\n    const opSection = nextHeadingMatch\n      ? opRest.slice(0, nextHeadingMatch.index)\n      : opRest.slice(0, 5000);\n\n    // Extract API URL\n    const urlMatch = opSection.match(/<code>\\s*((?:GET|POST|DELETE|PUT)\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\n    const apiUrl = urlMatch ? stripHtml(urlMatch[1]) : null;\n\n    // Extract parameters table\n    const parameters = parseTables(opSection);\n\n    // Extract code examples\n    const codeBlocks = extractCodeBlocks(opSection);\n    const requestExample = codeBlocks.find(b => b.includes(\"curl\")) || null;\n    const responseExample = codeBlocks.find(b => b.includes(\"resourceType\") || b.includes(\"HTTP/1.1\")) || null;\n\n    operations.push({\n      name: `${opType} ${resourceName}`,\n      type: opType,\n      apiUrl,\n      parameters,\n      requestExample,\n      responseExample,\n    });\n  }\n\n  // Special case for Medication which only has \"get\"\n  if (resourceName === \"Medication\" && operations.length === 0) {\n    const opPattern = /id=\"get-medication\"/;\n    const opMatch = html.match(opPattern);\n    if (opMatch) {\n      const opIdx = opMatch.index!;\n      const opRest = html.slice(opIdx);\n      const nextH = opRest.match(/<h[12]\\s/);\n      const opSection = nextH ? opRest.slice(0, nextH.index) : opRest.slice(0, 3000);\n\n      const urlMatch = opSection.match(/<code>\\s*(GET\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\n      operations.push({\n        name: \"get Medication\",\n        type: \"get\",\n        apiUrl: urlMatch ? stripHtml(urlMatch[1]) : null,\n        parameters: parseTables(opSection),\n        requestExample: null,\n        responseExample: null,\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  resources.push({\n    name: resourceName,\n    usCorProfile,\n    description,\n    operations,\n  });\n}\n\n// Extract OAuth scopes\nconst scopeSections = html.match(/<h3[^>]*id=\"patient-scope\"[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/);\nconst patientScopes = scopeSections ? parseTables(scopeSections[0]) : [];\n\nconst userScopeSections = html.match(/<h3[^>]*id=\"user-scope\"[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/);\nconst userScopes = userScopeSections ? parseTables(userScopeSections[0]) : [];\n\nconst systemScopeSections = html.match(/<h3[^>]*id=\"system-scope\"[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/);\nconst systemScopes = systemScopeSections ? parseTables(systemScopeSections[0]) : [];\n\n// Extract Bulk Export info\nconst bulkExportSection = extractBetween(html, /<h1\\s+id=\"bulk-export\"/, /<\\/div>\\s*<div class=\"dark-box\">/);\nconst bulkExportOps: { name: string; apiUrl: string | null; parameters: Array<Record<string, string>>; description: string | null }[] = [];\n\nfor (const op of [\n  { id: \"start-export\", name: \"Start Bulk Export\" },\n  { id: \"get-status\", name: \"Get Bulk Export Status\" },\n  { id: \"delete-export\", name: \"Delete Bulk Export Request\" }\n]) {\n  const opPattern = new RegExp(`<h2\\\\s+id=\"${op.id}\"[^>]*>`);\n  const opMatch = html.match(opPattern);\n  if (!opMatch) continue;\n\n  const opIdx = opMatch.index!;\n  const opRest = html.slice(opIdx + opMatch[0].length);\n  const nextH = opRest.match(/<h[12]\\s/);\n  const opSection = nextH ? opRest.slice(0, nextH.index) : opRest.slice(0, 3000);\n\n  const urlMatch = opSection.match(/<code>\\s*((?:GET|POST|DELETE)\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\n  const descMatch = opSection.match(/<p>([^<]*(?:export|Fetches|Deletes)[^<]*)<\\/p>/i);\n\n  bulkExportOps.push({\n    name: op.name,\n    apiUrl: urlMatch ? stripHtml(urlMatch[1]) : null,\n    parameters: parseTables(opSection),\n    description: descMatch ? stripHtml(descMatch[1]) : null,\n  });\n}\n\n// Extract CCDA section\nconst ccdaSection = extractBetween(html, /<h1\\s+id=\"ccda\"/, /<h1\\s+id=\"bulk-export\"/);\nconst ccdaUrlMatch = ccdaSection.match(/<code>\\s*(GET\\s+\\/[^<]+)<\\/code>/);\nconst ccdaParams = parseTables(ccdaSection);\n\n// Build output\nconst output = {\n  source: \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/fhir/index.html\",\n  extractionDate: new Date().toISOString().split(\"T\")[0],\n  fhirVersion: \"4.0.1\",\n  baseUrl: \"https://ehr2.charmtracker.com/api/ehr/v2/fhir\",\n  summary: {\n    totalResources: resources.length,\n    resourceNames: resources.map(r => r.name),\n    totalOperations: resources.reduce((sum, r) => sum + r.operations.length, 0),\n    resourcesWithUsCorProfile: resources.filter(r => r.usCorProfile).length,\n  },\n  resources,\n  oauthScopes: {\n    patient: patientScopes,\n    user: userScopes,\n    system: systemScopes,\n  },\n  bulkExport: {\n    description: \"Exports data of more than one resource of the group of patients in NDJSON format\",\n    operations: bulkExportOps,\n  },\n  ccda: {\n    description: \"Export medical records of a patient in HL7 CCDA format\",\n    apiUrl: ccdaUrlMatch ? stripHtml(ccdaUrlMatch[1]) : null,\n    parameters: ccdaParams,\n  },\n  parsingStats: {\n    inputFile: \"fhir-api-documentation.html\",\n    inputSizeBytes: html.length,\n    resourcesParsed: resources.length,\n    resourcesExpected: RESOURCE_NAMES.length,\n    parseFailures: RESOURCE_NAMES.filter(n => !resources.find(r => r.name === n)).map(n => ({\n      resourceName: n,\n      reason: \"Header not found in HTML\"\n    })),\n  }\n};\n\nwriteFileSync(OUTPUT_FILE, JSON.stringify(output, null, 2));\nconsole.log(`Extracted ${resources.length} resources with ${output.summary.totalOperations} {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_017z2jKNK8UwBYjHrMV1ADia","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Jrwh3wbejdxp4n8qkMv7kE","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/medicalmine-inc--charmhealth-ehr/ehi-export-report.md","content":"# MedicalMine Inc. (CharmHealth EHR) — EHI Export Documentation\n\nCollected: 2026-02-15\n\n## Source\n- Registered URL: https://www.charmhealth.com/ehr/electronic-health-information-export.html\n- CHPL IDs: 9741\n- Developer: MedicalMine Inc.\n- Product: CharmHealth EHR v1.2\n- Certification date: 2018-11-15\n\n## Navigation Journal\n\n**1. Initial probe:**\n```bash\ncurl -sI -L \"https://www.charmhealth.com/ehr/electronic-health-information-export.html\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n```\nReturned HTTP 200 with `Content-Type: text/html`, 10,141 bytes. Served from AmazonS3 via CloudFront. No redirects.\n\n**2. Fetched and examined the main page:**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/ehr/electronic-health-information-export.html\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o electronic-health-information-export.html\n```\nThe page is a static HTML page titled \"Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export\". It describes three export mechanisms:\n- **FHIR R4 API** for clinical data (linking to their API documentation)\n- **HL7 CCDA** export (via API and via the EHR UI)\n- **CSV export** for billing data (linking to resource center help pages)\n\n**3. Downloaded the FHIR API documentation:**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/fhir/index.html\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o fhir-api-documentation.html\n```\nA single-page API documentation site (420KB) documenting 24 FHIR R4 resources with list/get/search operations, OAuth authentication, Bulk Export API, and CCDA export API.\n\n**4. Downloaded encounter export instructions:**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/consultation/create-encounter.html\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o create-encounter.html\n```\nThe \"Create Encounter\" resource center page includes an \"Export\" section with screenshots showing how to export encounters as PDF, HL7 CCDA, or Surveillance Report from the EHR UI.\n\n**5. Downloaded export encounter screenshots from the resource center:**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/consultation/images/export-encounter.jpg\" -o export-encounter.jpg\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/consultation/images/export-encounter1.jpg\" -o export-encounter1.jpg\n```\nThese screenshots show the EHR encounter interface with export dropdown menus. First screenshot shows bulk export options (Export Encounters as PDF, Export Encounters as PDF (New), Export Encounters as HL7 CCDA) via the \"...\" (More Options) menu. Second screenshot shows per-encounter export options (Encounter as PDF, Encounter as HL7 CCDA, Surveillance Report - Registration, Encounter as PDF (New)) via the Export button.\n\n**6. Downloaded billing documentation pages:**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/billing/reports.html\" -o billing-reports.html\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/analytics/analytics.html\" -o analytics.html\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/billing/claims.html\" -o claims.html\ncurl -sL \"https://www.charmhealth.com/resources/billing/export-encounters-as-hl7-messages-for-billing-outside-charmhealth.html\" -o export-encounters-as-hl7-messages.html\n```\nThese are resource center help pages describing how to generate and export billing reports, claims data, insurance reports, and HL7 billing messages — all as CSV or PDF.\n\n**7. Took browser screenshots:**\n- Main EHI export page (full-page screenshot)\n- FHIR API documentation top section\n- Bulk Export section of FHIR API docs\n\n**8. Built enrichment script to extract structured data from FHIR API docs:**\nUsed Bun TypeScript to parse the 420KB HTML into a queryable JSON with all 24 resources, 68 operations, parameters, OAuth scopes, Bulk Export API details, and CCDA API details.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nCharmHealth documents a **multi-format EHI export approach** with three mechanisms:\n\n### 1. FHIR R4 API (Clinical Data)\n\nThe CharmHealth API Program exposes a comprehensive FHIR R4.0.1 API at `https://ehr2.charmtracker.com/api/ehr/v2/fhir`. It supports **24 FHIR resource types**:\n\n- AllergyIntolerance, Appointment, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, FamilyMemberHistory, Goal, Immunization, Location, MedicationAdministration, MedicationRequest, Medication, Observation, Organization, Patient, Practitioner, Procedure, Provenance, QuestionnaireResponse, RelatedPerson\n\n19 of 24 resources reference US Core profiles. Each resource supports list, get-by-ID, and search operations (with some exceptions: Medication has only get-by-ID; Provenance has only get-by-ID).\n\nAuthentication uses SMART on FHIR (OAuth 2.0) with support for:\n- Patient-facing applications (confidential and public clients)\n- Provider-facing applications\n- Backend services (JWT-based)\n- API Key authentication\n\nThe API supports `.well-known/smart-configuration` and a FHIR CapabilityStatement at the metadata endpoint.\n\n### 2. Bulk Export API (FHIR Bulk Data)\n\nThe FHIR Bulk Export API enables exporting data for groups of patients as NDJSON files:\n- `GET /Group/{group_id}/$export` — initiates export\n- `GET /$export-poll-status?_jobid=...` — checks export status, returns download URLs when complete\n- `DELETE /$export-poll-status?_jobid=...` — cancels an export\n\nThe response includes download URLs for each resource type (e.g., `Binary/Patient/...`, `Binary/Encounter/...`).\n\n### 3. HL7 CCDA Export\n\nAvailable via:\n- **API**: `GET /patients/{patient_id}/ccda` with optional date range filtering\n- **UI**: From the encounter dashboard, via \"Export\" button → \"Encounter as HL7 CCDA\" or via \"...\" menu → \"Export Encounters as HL7 CCDA\"\n\n### 4. CSV Billing Data Export\n\nBilling data can be exported as CSV from the EHR interface:\n- **Invoice & Receipts Reports**: Payment details, receipts, refunds, grouped by facility/patient/provider\n- **Insurance Reports**: Patient insurance details exportable as CSV\n- **Claims Reports**: Claims data including claims aging by payer, denied procedures, and detailed claim information\n- **Encounter Summary Reports**: Combined view of appointments, encounters, invoices, claims, and payments\n- **HL7 Billing Messages**: Encounters can be exported as HL7 messages for external billing systems\n\nEach billing report section in the resource center describes an \"Export as CSV\" button with options to export all details with subtotals or selected columns.\n\n### 5. PDF Export\n\nIndividual encounters can be exported as PDF from the UI, and billing reports can be exported as PDF with selectable layout (landscape/portrait).\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Clearly covered (via FHIR API):**\n- Patient demographics (Patient)\n- Allergies (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Conditions/diagnoses (Condition)\n- Medications and prescriptions (Medication, MedicationRequest, MedicationAdministration)\n- Lab results and vitals (Observation, DiagnosticReport)\n- Immunizations (Immunization)\n- Procedures (Procedure)\n- Care plans and goals (CarePlan, Goal)\n- Care teams (CareTeam)\n- Encounters/visits (Encounter)\n- Documents and attachments (DocumentReference)\n- Family history (FamilyMemberHistory)\n- Appointments (Appointment)\n- Questionnaire responses (QuestionnaireResponse) — covers intake forms, pre-screening\n- Related persons/contacts (RelatedPerson)\n- Clinical notes (likely via DocumentReference or embedded in Encounter)\n\n**Covered via CSV export (billing):**\n- Invoices and receipts\n- Insurance information\n- Claims data\n- Payment records\n- Denied procedures\n- Encounter summaries with billing details\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Custom/specialty clinical data**: CharmHealth has particular traction with integrative/functional medicine practices. Drug-herb interaction data, supplement orders (FullScript integration), and custom template content may not map cleanly to standard FHIR resources. It's unclear if these specialized data types are captured in the FHIR export or only in the CCDA/PDF exports.\n- **Telehealth session records**: The product includes integrated telehealth with recording capability, consent documents, and session metadata. These are not represented in any obvious FHIR resource.\n- **Secure messaging (CharmConnect)**: Provider-to-provider and provider-to-patient encrypted messages are a significant data store. No FHIR resource or export mechanism is documented for this.\n- **Inventory data**: Medication/supplement inventory tracking with stock levels and dispensing records — not covered in the FHIR API.\n- **AI Scribe transcriptions**: CharmAI Scribe generates clinical notes from ambient recordings. The raw transcriptions and AI-suggested codes may or may not be included in DocumentReference or encounter data.\n- **Images and image annotations**: The EHR supports image annotation tools, but it's unclear if annotated images are exported via DocumentReference.\n- **Flowsheet data**: The product supports flowsheets for tracking vitals and lab results across visits — likely captured in Observation, but not explicitly documented.\n- **Referral data**: The product has referral management; no clear export mechanism beyond what may be in DocumentReference.\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Question\n\nCharmHealth's EHI export page explicitly references the (b)(10) criterion and describes a **combined approach**: FHIR API for clinical data, CSV for billing data. This is **better than many vendors** because:\n\n1. They acknowledge that clinical FHIR data alone is insufficient for EHI export\n2. They separately document billing data export via CSV\n3. The resource list extends beyond strict US Core (includes Appointment, FamilyMemberHistory, MedicationAdministration, QuestionnaireResponse)\n\nHowever, the FHIR API portion is essentially the same as what would serve (g)(10) — the 24 resources documented are standard US Core resources plus a few extras. The \"EHI-specific\" part is the billing CSV export, which is documented only as user guide instructions for running reports from the UI, not as a programmatic bulk export mechanism.\n\nThe Bulk Export API appears to export only the 24 FHIR resource types, not billing or other non-clinical data. So the true (b)(10) export for a complete patient record would require:\n1. Running a FHIR Bulk Export (or individual patient API calls) for clinical data\n2. Manually running multiple billing reports from the UI and exporting as CSV\n3. Possibly exporting CCDA for any data not in the FHIR resources\n\nThis is not a unified \"export all EHI\" button — it's a collection of separate mechanisms that together could cover most data.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Clinical data**: FHIR R4.0.1 (JSON), conformant to US Core STU3.1.1 profiles for most resources. The API returns standard FHIR Bundle responses. Bulk export uses NDJSON per the FHIR Bulk Data spec.\n- **CCDA**: HL7 CCDA v2.1 XML export for clinical summaries\n- **Billing data**: CSV files from the UI (no programmatic API for billing data export)\n- **Encounters**: PDF export available from the UI\n\nThe FHIR format is appropriate for the clinical data it covers. FHIR R4 API (Clinical Data)\n\nThe CharmHealth API Program exposes a comprehensive FHIR R4.0.1 API at `https://ehr2.charmtracker.com/api/ehr/v2/fhir`. It supports **24 FHIR resource types**:\n\n- AllergyIntolerance, Appointment, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, FamilyMemberHistory, Goal, Immunization, Location, MedicationAdministration, MedicationRequest, Medication, Observation, Organization, Patient, Practitioner, Procedure, Provenance, QuestionnaireResponse, RelatedPerson\n\n19 of 24 resources reference US Core profiles. Each resource supports list, get-by-ID, and search operations (with some exceptions: Medication has only get-by-ID; Provenance has only get-by-ID).\n\nAuthentication uses SMART on FHIR (OAuth 2.0) with support for:\n- Patient-facing applications (confidential and public clients)\n- Provider-facing applications\n- Backend services (JWT-based)\n- API Key authentication\n\nThe API supports `.well-known/smart-configuration` and a FHIR CapabilityStatement at the metadata endpoint.\n\n### 2. Bulk Export API (FHIR Bulk Data)\n\nThe FHIR Bulk Export API enables exporting data for groups of patients as NDJSON files:\n- `GET /Group/{group_id}/$export` — initiates export\n- `GET /$export-poll-status?_jobid=...` — checks export status, returns download URLs when complete\n- `DELETE /$export-poll-status?_jobid=...` — cancels an export\n\nThe response includes download URLs for each resource type (e.g., `Binary/Patient/...`, `Binary/Encounter/...`).\n\n### 3. HL7 CCDA Export\n\nAvailable via:\n- **API**: `GET /patients/{patient_id}/ccda` with optional date range filtering\n- **UI**: From the encounter dashboard, via \"Export\" button → \"Encounter as HL7 CCDA\" or via \"...\" menu → \"Export Encounters as HL7 CCDA\"\n\n### 4. CSV Billing Data Export\n\nBilling data can be exported as CSV from the EHR interface:\n- **Invoice & Receipts Reports**: Payment details, receipts, refunds, grouped by facility/patient/provider\n- **Insurance Reports**: Patient insurance details exportable as CSV\n- **Claims Reports**: Claims data including claims aging by payer, denied procedures, and detailed claim information\n- **Encounter Summary Reports**: Combined view of appointments, encounters, invoices, claims, and payments\n- **HL7 Billing Messages**: Encounters can be exported as HL7 messages for external billing systems\n\nEach billing report section in the resource center describes an \"Export as CSV\" button with options to export all details with subtotals or selected columns.\n\n### 5. PDF Export\n\nIndividual encounters can be exported as PDF from the UI, and billing reports can be exported as PDF with selectable layout (landscape/portrait).\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Clearly covered (via FHIR API):**\n- Patient demographics (Patient)\n- Allergies (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Conditions/diagnoses (Condition)\n- Medications and prescriptions (Medication, MedicationRequest, MedicationAdministration)\n- Lab results and vitals (Observation, DiagnosticReport)\n- Immunizations (Immunization)\n- Procedures (Procedure)\n- Care plans and goals (CarePlan, Goal)\n- Care teams (CareTeam)\n- Encounters/visits (Encounter)\n- Documents and attachments (DocumentReference)\n- Family history (FamilyMemberHistory)\n- Appointments (Appointment)\n- Questionnaire responses (QuestionnaireResponse) — covers intake forms, pre-screening\n- Related persons/contacts (RelatedPerson)\n- Clinical notes (likely via DocumentReference or embedded in Encounter)\n\n**Covered via CSV export (billing):**\n- Invoices and receipts\n- Insurance information\n- Claims data\n- Payment records\n- Denied procedures\n- Encounter summaries with billing details\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Custom/specialty clinical data**: CharmHealth has particular traction with integrative/functional medicine practices. Drug-herb interaction data, supplement orders (FullScript integration), and custom template content may not map cleanly to standard FHIR resources. It's unclear if these specialized data types are captured in the FHIR export or only in the CCDA/PDF exports.\n- **Telehealth session records**: The product includes integrated telehealth with recording capability, consent documents, and session metadata. These are not represented in any obvious FHIR resource.\n- **Secure messaging (CharmConnect)**: Provider-to-provider and provider-to-patient encrypted messages are a significant data store. No FHIR resource or export mechanism is documented for this.\n- **Inventory data**: Medication/supplement inventory tracking with stock levels and dispensing records — not covered in the FHIR API.\n- **AI Scribe transcriptions**: CharmAI Scribe generates clinical notes from ambient recordings. The raw transcriptions and AI-suggested codes may or may not be included in DocumentReference or encounter data.\n- **Images and image annotations**: The EHR supports image annotation tools, but it's unclear if annotated images are exported via DocumentReference.\n- **Flowsheet data**: The product supports flowsheets for tracking vitals and lab results across visits — likely captured in Observation, but not explicitly documented.\n- **Referral data**: The product has referral management; no clear export mechanism beyond what may be in DocumentReference.\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Question\n\nCharmHealth's EHI export page explicitly references the (b)(10) criterion and describes a **combined approach**: FHIR API for clinical data, CSV for billing data. This is **better than many vendors** because:\n\n1. They acknowledge that clinical FHIR data alone is insufficient for EHI export\n2. They separately document billing data export via CSV\n3. The resource list extends beyond strict US Core (includes Appointment, FamilyMemberHistory, MedicationAdministration, QuestionnaireResponse)\n\nHowever, the FHIR API portion is essentially the same as what would serve (g)(10) — the 24 resources documented are standard US Core resources plus a few extras. The \"EHI-specific\" part is the billing CSV export, which is documented only as user guide instructions for running reports from the UI, not as a programmatic bulk export mechanism.\n\nThe Bulk Export API appears to export only the 24 FHIR resource types, not billing or other non-clinical data. So the true (b)(10) export for a complete patient record would require:\n1. Running a FHIR Bulk Export (or individual patient API calls) for clinical data\n2. Manually running multiple billing reports from the UI and exporting as CSV\n3. Possibly exporting CCDA for any data not in the FHIR resources\n\nThis is not a unified \"export all EHI\" button — it's a collection of separate mechanisms that together could cover most data.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Clinical data**: FHIR R4.0.1 (JSON), conformant to US Core STU3.1.1 profiles for most resources. The API returns standard FHIR Bundle responses. Bulk export uses NDJSON per the FHIR Bulk Data spec.\n- **CCDA**: HL7 CCDA v2.1 XML export for clinical summaries\n- **Billing data**: CSV files from the UI (no programmatic API for billing data export)\n- **Encounters**: PDF export available from the UI\n\nThe FHIR format is appropriate for the clinical data it covers. The CSV billing export is ad-hoc (report-by-report, not a unified schema).\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The FHIR API documentation is comprehensive and well-structured — a single-page doc with 24 resources, clear API URLs, parameter tables, request/response examples, and authentication flows\n- Sample responses include realistic FHIR JSON with actual field values\n- OAuth/SMART documentation is thorough with multiple client types\n- The EHI export landing page clearly links to all relevant documentation\n- Screenshots show the actual UI export interface\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- No formal data dictionary — the documentation relies on standard FHIR resource definitions without documenting CharmHealth-specific extensions, value sets, or constraints\n- No sample export files (no example NDJSON from bulk export, no example CSV billing export)\n- Billing export documentation is user-guide-style help pages, not technical specifications — there's no schema for the CSV files, no column definitions, no data type specifications\n- No documentation of relationships between the FHIR data and the billing CSV data\n- The FHIR API documentation doesn't describe what data from CharmHealth maps to which FHIR fields, making it impossible to know if all stored data is represented\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **FHIR resources**: Documented at the API operation level (list/get/search) with query parameters, but not at the field level. The actual FHIR resource structure is documented only through sample responses. A developer would need to rely on the US Core profiles and sample responses to understand the data structure.\n- **Billing CSV**: No column definitions, no schema, no data types. Only described as \"you can export as CSV\" in the help pages.\n- **Value sets**: Not documented. Standard FHIR/US Core value sets are implicitly used but not enumerated.\n- **Relationships**: Not explicitly documented.
